**Four findings, with their dispositions.** **F1** — G3's textual half as R41 worded it was **not satisfiable**, by 018/019 or by anything else: thirteen `sqlite_master` objects differ in text and none in structure, nine of them predating 018 entirely and four of them 018's own rebuild, which under R35 must declare its DDL locally and under rebuild-then-rename must quote the table name. **R53** amends G3 accordingly. **F2** — `schema_structure_hash` sorts columns by name and therefore cannot see physical column order, which `extractions` and `papers` genuinely differ in; **R54** makes it class C row **C14**, owner S3c, first action a census of positional row access on those two tables. **F3** — `engine/migrations/README.md` still carried the C11 wording R37 corrected in `run()`'s docstring; fixed in this commit. **F4** — nothing held `review.db` open at any gate, but its `-shm` was touched repeatedly during the session by ordinary `mode=ro` opens (a read-only open takes byte-range locks in the shared-memory file). Recorded as a **note for any future exclusivity gate**: `-shm` mtime is not evidence of a write, and `lsof`/`fuser` are the check.

*Addendum, 2026-09-23 (R85, MANIFEST-01 P3ii):* the exclusivity check before a live write is made against **scheduled jobs that read or write `review.db` or the evidence-engine tree** — the crontab and systemd timers that name either — not against every timer on the box: `sysstat-collect.timer` fires every 10 minutes, so a "nothing due within 30 minutes" clause can never be met. `lsof`/`fuser` immediately before the backup and before the write remain the check on open handles.

**The file grew 101,978,112 → 134,361,088 B**, +31 MB, because 018's two rebuilds free pages that SQLite does not return to the filesystem. **R55 rules no `VACUUM`**: the slack is free pages, every content hash is unchanged, and a compaction would be a live write like any other — rehearsed, backed up, fingerprinted — not a tidy-up. **Retention under R55:** the effective-result-02 pre-seed restore point, the Phase 3 rehearsal target and the Phase 3(i) fresh reference database are **retired**, each fingerprinted immediately before deletion and each matching; the **pre-write restore point is retained through sessions 7–9 and retires at the freshman freeze (session 10)**. It is now the only copy of the database as it stood before 018 and 019. See the retention ledger under Step 4.

**The R77/R78 audit.** The brief's run-link CHECK `(run_id IS NOT NULL OR run_marker = 'pre-manifest')` **admitted a row with both columns NULL** — measured by a reproducer, because SQLite passes a CHECK that evaluates to NULL. **Arms, runs and the writer.** `engine/core/run_manifest.py` writes the manifest and its stage rows before the first call, pins every named arm at its first manifest, and refuses a dirty tree, a pre-manifest, retired or pin-mismatched arm, a digest failure, and a cloud arm the spec does not enable — each before anything is written. The event writer requires `run_id`, accepts `run_marker` only from a migration (applied 017 still seeds, recognised by its file — row C22), and refuses claims on pre-manifest (R59), retired (R21) and run-unpinned (R10) arms; reviewer events on pre-manifest claims stay allowed under a `review_session` run, so rule row 7 is reachable on fixtures. `run_pipeline` opens a manifest instead of writing `review_runs` (R73). **Consequence, by design:** `run_pipeline` and `run_cloud_extraction` refuse at run open on any database without 020 — on live until 7b applies it.

**Four ledger items the Phase 1a read-out falsified**, each reported and not adapted around:
- *I3, partly:* `run_pipeline` already opens and activates an `extraction` manifest around the extract stage. Only the side runners extract without one. No `run_id` reaches `extract_paper`, whose own `run_id` parameter is a unit-map directory name (hence R116).
- *I4:* `contract_unmet` and `duplicate_detected` are already admitted by `field_events`' CHECK, and the reader already emits rows 15 and 6. **All eight S5b states are derivable without a migration.** Two gaps remain. Row 6 fires per `claim_id`, so two separate claims on a pinned arm resolve newest-wins silently (fork F4). And `duplicate_detected` is read by nothing.
- *I5, partly:* the retry budget's constant lives in `completeness.py`; its loop lives in the extractor and the cloud base.
- *I6:* the `extraction_models` block already exists in the spec model and the live spec, and reaches the call through the resolver. S5d's premise, "the live spec gains an extraction_models block", was already met on disk.

**The truncate measurement (Phase 1b M1, Phase 1c).** On Ollama 0.21.0 (`llama3.2:3b`, `num_ctx` 1024, an 8,425-token prompt):
- A top-level `"truncate": false` returns **HTTP 400** `the input length exceeds the context length`.
- No key, `truncate: true`, and `truncate` inside `options` all return 200, silently truncated to `prompt_eval_count` 1024. The last is logged `invalid option provided option=truncate`.
- ollama-python 0.6.1 and 0.6.2 cannot send the key. `ChatRequest` ignores extra fields, and the only route is the private `Client._request`, which bypasses the R56 capture.
- Consequence (R120, R127): S3f-min is the input-fit guard. Row 9's "truncate: false on every call" is amended by the addendum under the session table, and **D6 closes**. The "I13" that R62 named is the MANIFEST-01 Phase 1 read-out's own ledger item, not Step 2 row I13 (R8), and it is answered by M1.
- `run_calls` has no outcome column, so refused calls are recorded by paper event (R121; row C24 for session 10).

**The LOW_YIELD ruling (R136).** LOW_YIELD's absence set is the codebook's: `absence_sentinels` plus the non-value tokens, which the codebook derives from its two token keys and does not declare as a set (slice 1 I6). **Lessons.**
- A test's id is a caller's contract too. Three gates in this session named an exact id delta, and each was off by an id the brief had not seen: a parametrized case over a retired module (C3), a helper test whose subject lived in the retired module (C2), and a key-set pin on the stage's return value (C7). The collected-id diff found each, and the brief's "explain by id" clause absorbed each without a round trip.
- A docstring is a claim about the code. CC wrote in `test_atomic_terminal_write.py` that the event writer's one-transaction property was "tested there", and a grep before commit found no such test. The docstring now names the gap (B10).
- Retiring a writer is safe only after its readers move (R112). The first issue of R157 ordered them the other way and the amendment corrected it before code. The same check found R161's premise wrong: the helper it would have changed reads the rows, it does not write them.

**Architect errors this session**, in the same voice as CC's own:
- The 9c-P1 brief carried a stop-clause conflict. Its ledger said "If any INFERRED item is false, STOP", and its constraints said a contradiction "does not halt a read-only phase" (R65). When I4 proved false, CC finished the census outside the repository and held the commit; the ruling chose R65. A ledger item and a gate in one brief must agree.
- R157 as issued ordered the `ReviewDatabase` writers ahead of the readers they fed (R112 backwards). Corrected by amendment.
- R161 asserted that `mirror_legacy_into_events` writes legacy rows; it only reads them. Withdrawn.
- R162 quoted "nine B5 tests from session 5" for the audit-adjudication refusal without checking HEAD. There were three.
- The P0 brief gave the xfail's path as `tests/test_legacy_equivalence.py`. It is `tests/analysis/provenance/test_legacy_equivalence.py`.
- The P1 brief's I6 said the gate "collects 2,876 ids". That is pytest's total before deselection; the list holds 2,859.
- The C1 brief's I2 premised an event-writer capture for tests that fake `extract_paper` itself, so no success event exists to capture.
- The C7 brief's I3 premised a run-level telemetry sink in `extraction_telemetry.py`; there was none, and R172 built one.

CC's own:
- The C5 docstring claim of test coverage, caught before commit.
- Two `pipeline.md` atomic-writes lines naming the retired human-review and extraction-cleanup paths, missed in C2 and C3 and removed in C5.

**Lessons.**
- A trigger is a fact about a table that a brief can plan against without seeing. 9d-C2-R1's clause 3 planned an `UPDATE` on `run_stage_configs`, which migration 020 makes record-only. The ruling carried its own fallback, so the loss was one line. It is still the third brief this lane has written against a table's shape without reading its DDL first.
- A required keyword finds every caller at once. Making `run_id` and `arm` required turned three unnamed call lines in C2, and `test_export_all`'s fixture arm in C3, into `TypeError` / `UnknownArm` at the first run. A default would have let them keep reading the wrong thing quietly.
- A docstring written before its test can overclaim. C1's first draft said "only the latest live claim of each cell". That is not quite rule v2.1, and no test in the commit proved it. It was corrected before the gate the commit rests on.

**Architect errors this session**, in the same voice as CC's own:
- The C2 brief's I2 text and its CONSTRAINTS clause disagreed on the same edit: `__init__.py` was not a named file. CC stopped at the clean tree.
- R179 as issued put a code retirement (`normalize_prefix`) inside a docs-only classification commit. Amended by 9d-C4.
- The C4 brief's I2 said `normalize_prefix` had four tests (it has five), and its I3 said four blocker files (there are six).
- R177's "extract_*" wording excluded `elicitation_pass1`, which the writer's own stage set includes. Corrected by 9d-C2-R1 clause 4.
- The C2 report's note that a shared arm accessor was warranted was withdrawn at C3. Three sites read one spec field, and an accessor would have been a second place to spell it.
- The 9d-P0/PA brief's I3 premised that `iter_grid` requires `arms=`. It defaults to every registered arm. The C1 brief's I2 then premised that it yields nothing for an unregistered arm; it raises.

CC's own:
- The C1 docstring's "only the latest live claim", caught and corrected before the gate.
- The first closeout edit script matched `| B14 | ` against both the Step 2 row and the coverage-map line. Its uniqueness assert refused before any write.

**Lessons.**
- A seam compared as sets is a census of the fixtures, not only of the data. The C-P1 seam found a one-sided fixture (INGESTED on status, eligible on events) in a test PRISMA was never its subject. A count comparison would have passed it.
- Retiring a test id should retire its helpers in the same commit. C-P1 retired `TestPrismaLowYield` and left `test_low_yield._advance_to_ai_audit`, which still called all four writers, with no caller until 3b.
- A drift pin on line numbers fires on any deletion above an entry point. The first gate run caught it both times, and regenerating in the same commit kept each commit a green tree.
- A retirement note that names the retired identifier fails the grep gate that proves the retirement. Name the ruling, not the symbol.
- Read the DDL before trusting a vocabulary premise. SE-A's reason-CHECK read reversed a plan premise before any migration text was drafted.

**Lessons.**
- **A guard scoped to an activation is reset by the next activation; scope guards to the run.** B15's first landing (10a-C9) stashed its field set in a ContextVar `activate()` overwrote on every call — correct in isolation, silently defeated the moment a second, unguarded activation of the same run nested inside it, which is exactly `run_extraction`'s own shape. R225a's fix scopes the guard to the thing it protects (the run), not to the mechanism that happens to invoke it.
- **An opt-in guard is a guard that quietly does not fire**, kept beside the lesson above as its general case: R223a (FT screening never opens a manifest, so the override-refusal never activates there) and R225's original design (nothing opted in until `run_pipeline.py` did, and even then incompletely) are two more instances in the same session.
- **The 9e-SE-A §2 table already answered 10a-C7's I5** — read the read-out before restating the plan's own stage list as fact; the plan can go stale against a read-out committed after it.
- **A mutation that disables a self-check is invisible to a test that re-verifies independently**, not to the check's own record: every mutation note this session (B15, C31, C22 exempted per its brief, R224a's chain construction, R223a's comparison, C10's registry reset) restored byte-identical and was proven by re-running the specific test the mutation targeted, not by trusting the diff.
- **/loop wakeups this session: none.** `ScheduleWakeup`/`/loop` were never invoked; every wait on a long-running gate used a background Bash artifact-wait (`until grep -qE 'passed|failed|error' ...`), per the standing "never poll via `pgrep -f`" rule. The plan's assumption of "none configured" for this lane is **confirmed**, not corrected.

**Architect errors** this session, in the same voice as CC's own:
- 10a-C1's I1 asserted a migration CLI from purpose; none existed (R222a).
- 10a-C7's I5 asserted an FT stage row from the plan's own stage list, against the 9e-SE-A §2 read-out already in hand in the same session (R223a).
- 10a-C8-B's ledger anchor `:711` named a line inside `_restart_ollama_and_retry`, not `ollama_chat` — a citation-by-line-number error the project's own "cite by content anchor" rule exists to prevent.
- 10a-C2's I2 guessed `run_manifests.run_id` as TEXT; it is INTEGER — corrected before the DDL was drafted, "held with a correction."
- 10a-CLOSE's own M2 (this brief) restated 10a-C9's A2 census, which itself missed `engine/agents/extractor.py`'s nested `active_run` call — a report's census is INFERRED at the next brief, not settled fact, the same lesson R209/R201 already carry for counts.

CC's own:
- 10a-C2: a test's expected screening-import count needed correcting mid-task after the fixture's own construction was read more carefully (the `data_root=fresh.parent` bug, corrected before the gate).
- 10a-C6-B: T3's first draft (forcing a `KeyError` from `docling`) didn't reach the unclassified-exception path at all — `docling` succeeded and the error was silently absorbed downstream; redesigned to patch the write-phase function instead.
- 10a-C8-B: the mock-shape ripple — six existing test files (`test_extractor.py`, `test_extraction_events.py`, `test_citation_guard.py`, `test_elicitation_pipeline.py`, plus two more mocks inside `test_extractor.py`) needed their `ollama_chat`/pass-function mocks updated to the `(value, hash)` return shape, found only by running the full gate, not by any targeted file list.
- 10a-C9: T10's first draft merely repeated T9's shape with different narration, which would not have distinguished "checks the direct caller" from "searches the whole stack"; redesigned to route the write through a test-file wrapper called *from* a migrations-dir module, the structurally distinct case the ruling actually needed proven.
- 10a-CLOSE: found, before writing Part 1's test, that `engine/agents/extractor.py:932`'s nested `active_run` call silently defeated B15 in every real production run — verified empirically (a live contextvar-nesting reproduction) before reporting it, then stopped per CONTRADICTION HANDLING rather than adapt around it; became R225a.

**Lessons.**
- **A CHECK written as excluded states passes on NULL — found by rehearsal read-back, not by the tests** that pinned the excluded states. Rule 11's "tested with a NULL in every column it names" is the test that would have caught it.
- **A premise carried forward as READ is still a claim about the code.** "J6 blocks export" was true of a workflow state live no longer has; the census found `get_current_blocker` returns the first pending stage, and live's is `PDF_ACQUISITION`.
- **The venue is part of the experiment.** A live smoke would have pinned the arm Run 7 will use and overwritten the Run-6 exports; the copy proved the same write path with the same pin tuple (C1a) and wrote nothing live.
- **Evidence placed in the tracked tree can refuse the run it documents** (`open_run`'s dirty-tree check, R241(i)).
- **A generated artifact travels with the code that generates it:** the inventory pin turned the first C2 gate red (R241(ii)) — and, at this closeout, the retained throwaway review would have turned it red again (R241(iv), B21).

**What changed in the engine.** A run's manifest now always ends: a gate stop records which gate, an interrupt or SIGTERM/SIGHUP records which signal (rolling back any uncommitted write first), and an interrupted command line exits at once rather than waiting out the abandoned model call, which Ollama then cancels. A manifest records the engine state tagged at HEAD. The extraction mapping no longer carries a branch that could not run. CLAUDE.md is true at HEAD on importers, the lifecycle and the gate. No schema change, no live write, no tag.

**What changed in the engine.** The arm pin now covers every template an extraction stage can send: an edit to the elicited priming wrapper, the per-field evidence formatting or the attempt-2 feedback block moves the stage's `prompt_hash` and refuses a run against the old pin. What Run 7 sends is byte-identical (three literals recorded on the pre-change tree). No extraction, audit, parse or quality-check entry point falls back to spec-less defaults any more: `cfg` (or `spec`) is required, and the PARSE stage threads the run's spec to the parser, so the request matches the manifest's resolution. A paper whose parsed text is refused at selection gets the same `extraction_failed` event a refusal in the loop gets, and leaves PRISMA's `extraction_in_progress`. Dead code retired; an unfillable elicited sentinel is a named refusal. No schema change, no live write, no tag.

**Architect errors that cost a stop** — premises restated from a ruling, a report or a summary instead of read.

- 12c-C53 I6 assumed `parse_pdf` had no caller beyond `parse_all_pdfs` and tests; `reparse_papers` was one (R418).
- Predictions that broke and were reported, not stops: E-PASS2-A's (`SYSTEM_PASS2` never sent; the composition predates the write path); C52-A's P2 (`vision_opts` carries a cfg, spec-less one hop up — C53); D21-A's P1 (`NoParsedText` has a code).

**What changed in the engine.** The audit stage locates each claim against the parsed-text version it was extracted from, not the paper's newest, and a `citation_located` event must name that same text or the writer refuses it. A model extraction claim must carry its `extraction_uid`, so its input identity can no longer be stored under a NULL key. When a paper is extracted again on a new text, the new outcome — stored, incomplete, failed or refused at selection — supersedes every old-text claim of the arm on that paper; a cell left without a live claim reads missing. `semantic_verify` no longer takes the paper text it never sent, and the documents say what the auditor checks: the value against the extractor's snippet. No request changed (every stage row and both pins identical to `ad01226`), no schema change, no live write, no tag.

**Gate by collected id.** 3,153 at open → 3,162 (D23, +9) → 3,164 (the strict check, +2) → 3,167 (D26, +3) → 3,167 (B23, one test rewritten under its id) → 3,168 (keyword-only, +1) → 3,177 (D24, +9). Seven green gate runs before this closeout's, 766.48–787.36 s, one at open and one before each commit. One deliberate red run: the strict citation check before any fixture edit (75 failed, 30 errors, all `CitationTextMismatch`), run to list the affected sites.

**What changed in the engine.** A run sends only what its manifest declared: under an active run, a model call whose stage has no row in the manifest, or whose row names a different model, is refused before anything reaches the server and before anything is recorded; in the extraction loop that refusal stops the run rather than failing a paper. A run that screens declares its screening model's preflight, so a pipeline started at search or screen can now run its own SCREEN stage; an undeclared preflight reaches the run as itself, not as a model that failed to load. A start at or before SCREEN stops at the adjudication gate whether or not `--skip-to` named it. The audit-review gate stops on the audit review's own state: a run that has extracted and audited ends `('interrupted', "blocked:audit_review")` and exports nothing until `AUDIT_REVIEW_COMPLETE` is complete — on the validation review as well, where `PDF_ACQUISITION` is pending. No request changed for a declared call (every stage row and both pins identical to `ad01226`), no schema change, no live write, no tag.

## Decision log

Rulings made by the PI in the architect session of 2026-09-19 to 2026-09-21, in order. The architect's four provisional rulings of 2026-09-19 (recall-bounded filter with code-enforced basis; SYNERGY as reference; a tiered lane with 2d first; a 50 KB plan) were withdrawn after the external review and replaced by R1–R9 below.

| Date | Ruling | Made by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-19 | Step 1 scope approved as proposed: one read-only CC session, startup verify + fingerprint, D1 then D2 (brief DISCOVERY-01 issued) | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | Push 7e09de3 immediately; Part B proceeds with five added items (D2-12…D2-16) | Architect, within the approved scope |
| 2026-09-20 | All human decisions on disk were made by the PI without Claude; their quality is bounded by what the queue artifact presented (J5) | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | The engine must be operable without Claude; human-in-the-loop steps need an interface (class J added) | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R1 — REJECT means withdraw: no value in the current result, original kept in history, correction is a separate act | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R2 — A duplicated field is a failure with the missing-field retry budget, then goes to a person unresolved; unexpected fields are dropped and logged | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R3 — A stale workbook is refused and the superseding extraction named; re-presentation belongs to the app | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R4 — Eligibility and processing outcome are separate facts; corpus = eligible; exports use an analysis-ready subset and report failures with a reason | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R5 — Evidence-backed exclusion with cross-family verification adopted as a pre-registered experiment; neither-in-nor-out records go to full text by default; adoption bar ≤1% of final inclusions lost per review with the 95% upper limit reported (Cochrane RCT Classifier standard, PMID 33171275; dual-human 97.5% as practice comparator, PMID 31972274); benchmark pools \~300 held-out final inclusions | PI, number from literature |
| 2026-09-20 | R6 — Cloud arms opt-in per run, declared in the spec, recorded in the manifest, off by default | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R7 — Immutable history at paper and field level, a written resolution rule, one reader; built as event store + reader first, readers migrated one at a time | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R8 — Operator app: local Gradio on the DGX, one screen per decision, who and when recorded; audit review first | PI |
| 2026-09-20 | R9 — Run 7 runs on a named, tagged engine state (first: *freshman*), defined as a closed set of units with everything else open under stated limits; set to be argued in Step 4 | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R10 — Arms are declared per review in the Review Spec. Each arm has a name, a kind (model \| human extractor) and one pinned configuration; a changed model, prompt, codebook or options is a new arm, never a newer claim in the old one. Supersession within an arm happens only when that arm re-extracts a paper whose input identity changed (S3d). From session 7, a write whose configuration does not match its declared arm is refused. Existing arms (local, anthropic_sonnet_4_6, openai_o4_mini_high) are registered at migration with configuration "not recorded (pre-manifest)"; nothing is backfilled. Two claims on one cell that share a pre-manifest configuration do not supersede — the cell is unresolved (needs re-review) | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R11 — Actor role is recorded on every event: reviewer \| extractor (model or human). Rule rows that let a human decision override a claim fire only on reviewer events. Human extractor arms are independent arms, treated exactly like model arms | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R12 — Arms are data: an arm registry read by one reader with no per-arm code branches. The number and kind of arms vary by review (zero, one or several human extractor arms). Asking the PI or review architect which arms a review uses is an S7 review-setup requirement (recorded, not built); until then the spec declares them (block lands with S3a, session 7) | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R13 — Partial coverage: an arm may be assigned a subset of papers. A cell outside an arm's assignment is "not assigned" — returned by the reader as out of scope, not an S5b state, excluded from every denominator. "Missing" means assigned and absent. Model arms default to the full corpus. The assignment table is built with human arm loading (session 12), as a separate table | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R14 — Human extractor workbooks are loaded as arms in session 12 alongside the importer. `human_extractions` is created by a numbered migration with a receipt; the self-provisioning `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is retired; the TEXT "EE-NNN" key is reconciled to `papers.id` in that session's design | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R15 — Q5: S4 screening evidence is a separate table keyed to the event, added with S4; no S4 columns are reserved on the event tables | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R16 — Q3: `UNIQUE(paper_id, arm)` on `cloud_extractions` is dropped in session 6, in the same change that puts `load_arm` behind the reader | PI, on architect recommendation |
| 2026-09-21 | R17 — "Asserted with evidence" requires a citation-located event against the current claim. That event is written by one shared, deterministic locator (a single implementation of the auditor's normalize + exact/fuzzy match). Each located / not-located event records the parsed-text identity checked, the threshold, and the locator version. Without a located event, the state is "asserted without locatable evidence", and "snippet supplied: yes/no" is kept as provenance. The test is identical for values and sentinels, for every arm, and for human extractor source quotes. The elicited path writes the located event at extraction time. Legacy rows start with no located event. Upgrading them is a separate, dated locatability measurement after parsed-text identity is settled (S3e, session 8), recorded as new events, never as reconstructed history. Default at migration: no located events reconstructed from `audit_status` | PI, on architect recommendation |
| 2026-09-21 | R18 — Q1 refuse (two reviewer decisions on one claim → unresolved); Q2 keep row 3 with `value_unchanged` in provenance; Q4 arm is a required argument; Q6 reader ignores non-codebook fields now, deletion recorded as an event in session 12; Q7 auditor verdicts are provenance, not a field state, and flagged/contested spans count as work owed in derived stage completion; Q8 and Q9 recorded as state at migration; Q10 PI v2 audit is measurement only; A11 Option B (session 5 stops writing and marks deprecated, session 12 drops) | PI, on architect recommendation |
| 2026-09-21 | R19 — Operational invariant until S3d lands (session 8): no local extraction runs on corpus papers (the skip key cannot match; a run would re-extract all 190) | PI |
| 2026-09-21 | R20 — Every "unresolved" outcome names its exit, and an exit is a reviewer event. Duplicate values (v2 row 2) and indistinguishable pre-manifest claims (v2 row 5) are resolved by CORRECT or WITHDRAW whose against-reference names every competing claim; ACCEPT against such a cell is refused at write (it cannot say which value it endorses). Conflicting reviewer decisions (v2 row 3) are resolved by a reviewer event referencing all the competing decisions, which then evaluates as withdraw/correct/accept. Reviewer decision against a superseded claim (v2 row 4): the exit is a new decision against the current claim. Consequence for the schema: a reviewer event's against-reference is a set of claim ids (or of decision ids, for the conflict case); session 5 designs its representation | PI, on architect recommendation |
| 2026-09-21 | R21 — Arms are immutable once they hold a claim: an arm's configuration cannot be re-pinned (a new configuration is a new arm, per R10). Retiring an arm means it accepts no new claims; its claims and decisions stand. Resolves U1 | PI, on architect recommendation |
| 2026-09-21 | R22 — U2: a reviewer decision on a cell outside the arm's assignment is refused at write (session-12 importer requirement); the reader's row 0 is unchanged. U3: a cross-arm judgment is not an event shape; a reference standard, when Paper 1 needs one, is its own arm whose claims reference their source claims — not built in session 5. U4: accepted as correct behaviour; R19 is extended — no cloud extraction runs until session 7 pins configurations. U6: `located = false` and no located event stay one S5b state, distinguished in provenance. The 131 'No comparison reported' spans are ordinary values under v2.1; whether the phrase is a codebook sentinel is a codebook decision for session 9 | PI, on architect recommendation |

The S2 Phase 1 design is closed; session 5 (S2 Phase 2) may be briefed | PI |

Existing extractions, spans, cloud extractions, auditor verdicts, adjudication tables and workflow stamps are NOT imported — they remain in place, read-only, as a regression fixture and telemetry. | 2026-09-21 | R31 — Standing rule. While the engine is settling into *freshman*, a legacy artifact — file, script, path, table, or committed output — is retained only if it serves the engine going forward. Serving a publication is not a reason to retain. Legacy tables under R25 stay because they serve the engine (regression fixture; session-8 reuse-key and input-identity tests). The rule is applied to what a session touches, not to the whole tree; retirement is recorded in a ledger and executed in the session that owns the artifact | PI |

T1–T5 inject GitState(..., state_tags=…); T4 also asserts no manifest row is written; T5 asserts a dirty tree with two state tags raises DirtyTree, not the ambiguity refusal. Add T6: the real git_state() against a throwaway repo under tmp_path, tagged inside the test (one annotated `freshman` tag; then a second state tag), asserting the state_tags read. No tag in this repo is read for assertions or created. 5. manifest_sha256 covers engine_state; record this in the docstring as intended (the hash covers the recorded body)." *Transcribed:* `b05ef062f34af07f9eaf3d75efb1bedfbc13fb0b`; T6's setup uses `git init-db` (the suite fence refuses the word `init`); docstring placement fixed at `bcceff8897c9076a9f317ab8ad146aa1624e281b` | PI, on architect recommendation |
